**Enum values and triage bucket mapping:**

| `FailureLayer` | Value | Maps from diagnose_failure | Issue layer |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CONTEXT` | `"context"` | `stale_context`, `retrieval_failure`, `memory_miss`, `context_window_exceeded` | context (retrieval/memory) |
| `CONSTRAINT` | `"constraint"` | `hook_blocked_write`, `test_ratchet_violation`, `lane_tool_sandbox_violation`, `external_dependency_install`, `max_steps_exhausted` + `HARD_STOP` | constraint (guardrails) |
| `VERIFICATION` | `"verification"` | `wrong_implementation`, `ac_gap`, `wrong_interpretation`, `test_failure`, `gate_failed` | verification (feedback loops) |
| `PLANNING` | `"planning"` | `tool_discovery_failure`, `loop_detected`, `no_progress`, `orchestration_error` + `REASONING` | planning (orchestration) |
| `INFRASTRUCTURE` | `"infrastructure"` | All `ENVIRONMENTAL` causes: `stale_path`, `dirty_working_tree`, `timeout_exceeded`, etc. | (extends issue taxonomy) |

| `context` | `.datum/context/step-*.txt` | 1 day |
| `run` (config only) | `.datum/runs/` | 90 days |

<summary>Agent "AUTHOR B literal verification" completed</summary>
<result>Done. Here's the report:

---

**PR-B: Literal Verification — commit `9a8963d` on `feat/w2-author-verify`**

**Two new modules, 41 new tests, 628 total passing.**

---

**Verification pipeline steps (`verify_literals(proposal, fixture_dir) -&gt; LiteralVerdict`)**

1. **Synthesize module file** — `render_module_file(sketch, existing)`: new module returns sketch verbatim; append mode produces `existing.rstrip("\n") + "\n\n" + sketch`, matching the plan's append contract.
2. **Synthesize test file** — `render_test_file(module_path, names, cases)`: always emits `import pytest` + `from &lt;module_path&gt; import &lt;names&gt;`, then one `def &lt;tc.name&gt;(...):` per `TestCase`. `tmp_path` fixture is auto-detected by scanning setup/assertion text and added as a parameter when present.
3. **AST safety gate** — `check_ast_safety(source)` walks the AST and rejects: blocked imports (`subprocess`, `socket`, `ctypes`, `shutil`, `importlib`, `http`, `urllib`, …), blocked call names (`eval`, `exec`, `compile`, `__import__`, `breakpoint`), and SyntaxError (reported as a rejection, not an exception).
4. **Execute** — tempdir created; module written under `&lt;tmpdir&gt;/&lt;green_target&gt;` with `__init__.py` chain; test file written under `&lt;tmpdir&gt;/&lt;red_target&gt;`; a `conftest.py` placed **adjacent to the test file** (not the tmpdir root) that re-inserts tmpdir into `sys.path` — this is required because `python -I` strips sys.path before collection, but pytest processes conftest in the test directory before attempting module import. Subprocess: `[python, "-I", "-m", "pytest", test_file, "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]` with stripped env and `VERIFY_TIMEOUT_S=60` hard limit.
5. **Verdict** — `classify_pytest_output` parses pass/fail counts; `raw` capped at 2000 chars; `LiteralVerdict.passed` is `True` only when returncode 0 AND zero failures.

**AST gate behavior** — rejects before any subprocess is spawned; `safety_rejections` list is non-empty on any hit; `ran=0, failed=0` in the returned verdict (no execution attempted).

**Corrective re-ask shape** — `verify_literals` returns `LiteralVerdict(passed=False, failures=["test_wrong_assert"])` with the raw pytest one-liner. The caller (foreman/server, PR-C wiring) feeds this back as: `"test_wrong_assert: assert add(1, 2) == 999 failed against your sketch (got 3) — fix the assert OR the sketch so they agree"`. One literal re-ask budget, independent of the schema re-ask.

**Test delta** — +41 tests (19 in `test_literal_codegen.py`, 22 in `test_literal_check.py`). Covers: render correctness, append contract, AST gate (all blocked names/imports + allowed stdlib), full pipeline pass, wrong assert detection, safety gate blocking execution, append mode with existing fixture, timeout patch, `pytest.raises` in cases, `tmp_path` fixture in cases.

**Suite result** — `628 passed, 2 deselected` (the 2 deselected are `@pytest.mark.live` tests excluded by default). `ruff check` clean on all touched files.</result>

<summary>Agent "Issue 81 abort-on-dirty" completed</summary>
<result>Done. Here's the summary:

---

**What constitutes "dirty"**: Any `git status --porcelain` output line after excluding `.datum/` prefixed paths — covers modified tracked files (staged or unstaged), untracked files, and deletions. All standard porcelain v1 XY status codes are covered.

**Where the guard lives**: `datum/tdd_driver.py` — new module. The public API is `check_clean_working_tree(repo_path: Path, *, allow_dirty: bool = False)` which raises `DirtyWorkingTreeError` (a `RuntimeError` subclass) listing every dirty file path with a "commit or stash" remediation message. It's a standalone preflight function meant to be called before `agent_loop()`, not embedded inside the loop itself.

**`.datum/` exclusion**: `_git_status_porcelain` returns raw output; `check_clean_working_tree` filters out `.datum/` paths so datum's own runtime artefacts (transcripts, locks, run state) never trip the guard.

**Override mechanism**: `allow_dirty=True` kwarg suppresses the check entirely. The issue doesn't specify an override, but it's standard practice for CI environments that manage state externally.

**Test delta**: 15 new tests in `tests/test_tdd_driver.py` — all subprocess calls mocked via `patch("datum.tdd_driver._git_status_porcelain")`. Covers: clean tree (2), all dirty status codes (5), error message content (3), `allow_dirty` bypass (2), `.datum/` exclusion (2), `repo_path` routing (1).

**Suite result**: 15/15 new tests pass. 22 pre-existing failures in the worktree environment are unrelated (pydantic-core version mismatch, local LLM model issues) and present on the base commit before any changes.

**Commit SHA**: `ce3d0eb`</result>

**progress.json schema** (`/Volumes/Extra/repos/gitrdunhq/datum/.worktrees/w2-progress/datum/agent_loop.py`, class `_ProgressWriter`):

| Field | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `run_id` | string | `{phase}-{YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ}`, unique per episode |
| `phase` | string | e.g. `"act_red"`, `"act_green"` |
| `status` | `"running"` \| `"done"` \| `"escalated"` | terminal states are final |
| `steps_taken` | int | count of steps committed to `steps_log` |
| `current_objective` | string | first 200 chars of last `thought` |
| `completed_steps` | `[{"tool": str, "target": str}]` | accumulates `tool_result`, `plan_update`, `final_answer`, `context_compaction` events |
| `blockers` | `[str]` | first 120 chars of each `error`-event observation |
| `last_tool` | string | `tool_name` of most recent step |
| `last_event` | string | `StepEvent` discriminator of most recent step |
| `updated_at` | ISO-8601 UTC | timestamp of this write |
| `escalated` | bool | true when `status == "escalated"` |
| `reason` | string \| null | escalation reason (e.g. `"max_steps_exhausted"`, `"timeout_exceeded"`) |

**Write points in the loop:**
1. `progress.start(task)` — immediately after episode init, writes `status: running`
2. `progress.record_step(step_entry)` — at all five `steps_log.append` sites: truncated-thought error, unverified-done escalation, unverified-done rejection (continue), final-answer (done), and the main OBSERVE step at the bottom of the loop
3. `progress.finish(escalated, reason)` inside `_finish()` — writes `status: escalated` for all escalation paths; done is already written by `record_step` on `final_answer`

**How floor watcher can consume it:** poll `.datum/progress.json`; it's always valid JSON (atomic tmp→rename writes). Check `status` to know if the run is live (`running`), succeeded (`done`), or failed (`escalated`). Use `completed_steps` instead of parsing transcript JSONL. Use `blockers` to surface the last error observations. The `run_id` field ties back to the transcript filename (same phase + timestamp prefix).

**Test delta:** +6 new tests in `tests/test_agent_loop.py`:
- `test_progress_json_written_on_done` — file exists, `status=done`
- `test_progress_json_written_on_escalation` — file exists, `status=escalated`, reason present
- `test_progress_json_schema_fields` — all 12 required fields present, `run_id` contains phase
- `test_progress_json_completed_steps_accumulate` — two `read_file` + `done` in `completed_steps`
- `test_progress_json_error_step_goes_to_blockers` — invalid-tool rejection surfaces in `blockers`
- `test_progress_json_is_valid_json_during_run` — via `on_step` callback, each in-flight snapshot is readable valid JSON

**Suite result:** 146 passed, 1 failed (`test_compaction_emits_typed_transcript_record`) — that 1 failure is pre-existing on `main`, not introduced by this change.

<result>Done. Here's the summary:

---

**PR3 `corpus_sql` — implemented and committed on `feat/w2-corpus-sql` as `8168fd4`**

**What was built:**

`datum/memory/corpus_sql.py` — new module with:
- `sql_guard(query)` — pure string scanner; strips single-quoted strings, double-quoted identifiers, line and block comments, then: (1) requires SELECT/WITH start, (2) rejects semicolons outside literals, (3) scans for 19 forbidden tokens (`ATTACH`, `COPY`, `INSTALL`, `LOAD`, `PRAGMA`, `SET`, `CREATE`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `DROP`, `ALTER`, `EXPORT`, `IMPORT`, `CALL`, `READ_JSON`, `READ_CSV`, `READ_PARQUET`, `GLOB`, `GETENV`)
- `render_rows()` — deterministic table renderer with 50-row / 3800-char caps and omitted-rows hints
- `run_corpus_query()` — pipeline: guard → in-memory DuckDB → **materialise all file-backed sources as `TABLE` (not `VIEW`) before `SET enable_external_access=false`** (critical: DuckDB's lazy view evaluation means the lock must come after all file I/O is done) → execute → sanitize via `prompt_sanitizer`
- 6 tables: `transcripts`, `failures`, `run_state`, `lane_files`, `token_metrics`/`kv_state` (sqlite ATTACH+materialise+DETACH), `floor_runs`; missing sources → empty tables, never errors

**Wiring:**
- `scripts/lane-tools/corpus_sql.py` — lane tool script
- `manifest.toml` — entry for `corpus_sql` (network=false, read=`.`, 30s, epic 78)
- `agent_loop.py` `TOOL_CATALOG` — added `corpus_sql` entry
- `local_llm.py` `READ_ONLY_TOOLS` — added `"corpus_sql"`
- `datum/cli.py` — `datum corpus "&lt;query&gt;"` command

**Tests: 92 passed, 0 failed**
- Guard acceptance (10), empty/whitespace (3), must-start-SELECT (7), multi-statement (4), denylist-named-in-error (8 parametrized), denylist-rejected (12 parametrized), UNION extraction (2), comment injection (4), render_rows (8), inject_limit (4), strip_sql_strings (4), SHOW TABLES (2), guard rejection via `run_corpus_query` (7), all 6 views with fixture data (10), output sanitization (1), limit boundary (2)

**Key design decision:** Data is materialised into in-memory DuckDB tables before `enable_external_access=false` is set. `CREATE VIEW ... AS SELECT ... FROM read_json_auto(...)` defers file I/O to query time, which would fail after the lock. `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT` forces immediate materialisation, so the lock correctly applies only to the caller's query.</result>

- `fix_import_path(red_prompt, wrong_module, correct_module)` — rewrites first `from X import`
- `add_test_prefix(target_path)` — ensures `test_` prefix on filename
- `fix_target_file_mismatch(spec_toml, wrong, correct)` — replaces first occurrence in TOML text

**Key parser bug fixed:** pytest formats traceback stanza headers as `=== FAILED &lt;node&gt; ===` — the E-lines appear *inside* that stanza, after the header. The initial parser was resetting the E-line buffer on the section separator, losing the detail. The rewritten `_build_e_line_map` uses a stanza state machine: it extracts the node-id from the header line, accumulates E-lines inside the stanza, and flushes them when the next section separator or short-summary break is hit.</result>

**Skeleton types generated:** Python only (`.py` / `.pyi`). Non-Python files pass through unchanged. The module produces three kinds of skeletons from one AST-based pass:
- Imports (all `import` / `from … import` lines, including multiline)
- Signatures (function/async-def/class with decorators, multiline args supported; body replaced with `...`)

Falls back to an indent-heuristic scanner for files that fail to parse (keeps lines starting with `import`, `from`, `def`, `class`, `@`, top-level `=`, and opening docstrings).

- `select_files_to_compress` sorts by score descending, keeps the top `keep_full_count`, returns the rest as compression candidates

<summary>Agent "R4 retrieve_context" completed</summary>
<result>Done. Here's the report:

---

**Retrieval pipeline** (`datum/memory/retrieve.py`, 310 lines):

- `retrieve_context(query, *, collection=None, top_k=5, max_chars=3800, repo_root=None) -&gt; str` — public API, no model calls
- `_load_failures()` — reads `.datum/tdd-failure.json`, `.datum/failures/*.json`, `.datum/runs/*/tdd-failure.json`; flattens JSON records to text via `_flatten_json()`
- `_load_transcripts()` — reads `.datum/transcripts/*.jsonl`; extracts `think_raw[:1500]` + `observation[:500]` per step
- `_load_specs()` — reads `specs/*.toml` as raw text
- `_tfidf_retrieve()` — fits `TfidfVectorizer(max_features=384)` on the corpus, transforms query, delegates to `_cosine_topk()` for brute-force cosine top-k
- `_cosine_topk()` — numpy dot-product cosine over the sparse TF-IDF matrix, `argpartition` for efficiency
- RLIMIT_AS=512MB enforced at entry via `_enforce_memory_limit()` (best-effort, idempotent)

**Sanitization chain**: retrieved text passes through `strip_special_tokens → strip_invisible_unicode → strip_secrets` from `datum.prompt_sanitizer` before any output is returned. Applies to both content blocks and nothing is bypassed.

**Collection types**: `"failures"`, `"transcripts"`, `"specs"`, `"all"` (default). Each maps to distinct file patterns; unknown collection returns a guarded error string.

**Output format**:
```
[RETRIEVED CONTEXT — query: "import error" — top 2 of 12]
--- .datum/failures/20260611-red.json (score: 0.82) ---
&lt;sanitized content&gt;
--- .datum/transcripts/act_red.jsonl (score: 0.71) ---
&lt;sanitized content&gt;
```

**Test suite** (`tests/test_memory_retrieve.py`): 45 tests, 45 passed, 0.61s. Covers: `_flatten_json`, `_sanitize` (special tokens + secrets), `_cosine_topk`, `_tfidf_retrieve` (relevance + top_k), all three loaders (happy path + malformed input), and `retrieve_context` end-to-end (empty corpus, unknown collection, char cap, collection scoping, relevance ranking, injection stripping, output format).

**Key relevance test** (`test_relevance_import_error_higher_than_assertion`): query `"import error ModuleNotFoundError module missing"` ranks `import-fail.json` above `assertion-fail.json` — confirmed passing.

**Ruff**: clean on both files.

<summary>Agent "Issue 73 structured tool results" completed</summary>
<result>Done. Here's the summary:

---

**Result types introduced**

`ToolResultData` TypedDict in `datum/agent_loop.py`:
```python
class ToolResultData(TypedDict):
    status: Literal["ok", "error"]
    summary: str
    next_valid_actions: list[str]
```

**Which tools get structured results**

All tools that actually execute — any step where `step_event` is `"tool_result"` or `"plan_update"`. Guard-rejected steps (`event="error"`) get no `tool_result` because no tool ran.

**`next_valid_actions` narrowing logic**
- On error observation: write tools excluded (likely share root cause); read tools remain
- After a successful write: only `run_command`, `read_file`, `done` — verify or finish
- After a successful read: all tools except `read_file_range` (no need to range-read after full read)
- All other cases: full `allowed_tools` list unchanged

**Backward compat**

The `observation` string in `history[]` is untouched — model still reads a plain string. `tool_result` is an additive key on `step_entry`, transcript records, and `progress.json` (`last_tool_result` field). All existing consumers are unaffected.

**Test delta**

12 new tests covering: `_make_tool_result` status/summary/next_valid_actions logic (6 unit tests), `step_entry` carries `tool_result` on execution events (1), no `tool_result` on error events (1), `progress.json` `last_tool_result` field (1), transcript JSONL records carry `tool_result` (1).

**Suite result:** 156 passed, 1 pre-existing failure (`test_compaction_emits_typed_transcript_record` — stale assertion about compaction prefix text, fails identically on `main`).

<summary>Agent "O3 orchestrator MVP CLI" completed</summary>
<result>Done. Here's the full report:

---

**PR3 / O3 — Orchestrator MVP**

**Commit:** `3f19bef` on `feat/w3-orchestrator-mvp`

**Files added:**
- `/datum_local/orchestrator.py` — 490 lines
- `/tests/test_orchestrator.py` — 450 lines

---

**CLI flags**

```
python -m datum_local.orchestrator --queue queue.toml
    --dry-run          # validate + preflight, no subprocess, no git mutations
    --resume           # idempotent restart from existing journal
    --omlx-url URL     # default: http://localhost:12200
    --run-dir DIR      # default: .temp/orchestrator-runs/ next to the queue file
    -v / --verbose     # DEBUG logging to stderr
```

**Journal event vocabulary used**

`queue_started`, `health_check`, `spec_preflight`, `spec_started`, `driver_exit` (with `timed_out?`, `failure_phase?`), `triage_started`, `triage_decision`, `spec_finished` (status: `succeeded|parked|skipped`), `queue_halted`, `queue_finished`

**Circuit breaker**

`halt_after_consecutive_failures` (default 3) counts parked specs in a row. A succeeded spec resets the counter to 0. On breach, `queue_halted` is journaled with reason `"budget: consecutive_failures"` and the loop exits; remaining specs are never launched.

**Resume semantics**

On startup with `--resume`: reads the existing `journal.jsonl`, runs `reduce_state()` to find specs with status `INTERRUPTED` (i.e., `spec_started` without matching `driver_exit` — crash-mid-run), journals `triage_started → triage_decision(park) → spec_finished(parked)` for each, then continues the loop. If the journal already ends with `queue_finished`, returns immediately — no-op.

**oMLX health gate**

Before each spec: `probe_omlx()` called. If down, polls every 15 s up to `omlx_wait_s` (default 600 s). If still down after the wait, journals `queue_halted` with reason containing "oMLX unreachable" and exits. A driver is **never** launched against a dead oMLX server.

**Triage stub**

`triage(FailureBundle) -&gt; RepairAction` always returns `kind="park"`. The seam is a plain module-level function — PR5 replaces its body without touching the orchestrator.

**Test delta**

36 new tests in `tests/test_orchestrator.py` covering: happy path (3-spec run), partial failure, circuit breaker (exact count, reset-on-success), resume after interruption, double-resume no-op, dependency ordering (skip-on-dep-fail, run-after-dep-success, in-order), oMLX-down halt (no driver launched), oMLX recovery, cooldown sleep injection, SIGTERM/`request_halt`, dry-run (no driver, no git branch), journal vocabulary fields, `reduce_state` consistency on real journal output, `_omlx_health_loop` unit tests, triage seam, and `Journal` durability.

**Full suite result:** 795 passed, 0 failed (pre-existing 2 deselected by live marker).</result>

**Commit SHA:** `8714d85` on `feat/w3-vector-store`

**What was built (R2 scope):**

**1. `datum/memory/vector_store.py` — NumpyVectorStore**

Full API surface mirroring ChromaDB's VectorStore:
- `add(texts, embeddings, metadata)` — appends, injects `text` into metadata
- `upsert(ids, embeddings, metadatas)` — insert-or-replace by id
- `query(collection, query_embedding, top_k)` → `list[tuple[id, meta, score]]` cosine-sorted
- `delete(collection, ids)` → count deleted; `delete_collection()`
- `list_collections()` — scans `.npz` files on disk
- `upsert_with_dedup(threshold=0.95)` — near-duplicate gate

Persistence: `&lt;col&gt;.npz` (float32 vectors + unicode str ids, `allow_pickle=False`) + `&lt;col&gt;.meta.jsonl`. Atomic writes use temp-stem + rename (numpy's auto-appended `.npz` handled explicitly). `_cosine_topk` is pure numpy: argpartition O(N) + clamp to [-1,1], handles zero-norm rows/query.

**2. `datum/memory/generic_chunker.py` — GenericChunker**

Dispatches on extension: `.md` → header-aware split on `#`/`##`/`###`; `.toml` → `[section]` split; everything else → fixed-window. Both structured paths include preamble capture and recursive windowing on long sections. `max_chars=1200`, `overlap=150`. Deterministic `chunk_id = sha256(source:section:text)[:16]`. `KnowledgeChunker` left entirely untouched.

**3. `datum/memory/ledger.py` — IngestionLedger**

SQLite WAL store satisfying `LedgerProtocol`. Schema: `(path, sha256) PRIMARY KEY` with `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. `is_ingested(path, sha256)` → same-path / different-hash = new version, not deduped. `stats()` for CLI display. Lazy connection; `close()` idempotent.

**4. `datum/memory/rag_engine.py` — RAGEngine rewired**

`_get_vector_store()` now returns `NumpyVectorStore(store_dir/numpy_index/)`. All existing `index/query/needs_reindex/reindex_all/delete_chunks` methods preserved. New `search(query_text, collection, top_k)` method for general corpus retrieval (any collection name, not just `reviewer_*`). Provider-mismatch guard covers both `query()` and `search()`. Error message updated from `datum rag-reindex` → `datum memory reindex`.

**5. CLI: `datum memory ingest` + `datum memory search`**

- `datum memory ingest [--collection docs] [paths...]` — walks files, chunks, embeds, ledger-deduped upsert into `.datum/index/`. Defaults to `docs/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PROPERTIES.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` when no paths given.
- `datum memory search "&lt;query&gt;" [--collection docs] [--top-k 5]` — loads provider from `.datum/index/`, calls `RAGEngine.search()`, renders scored results with source+section provenance. Graceful "not indexed" message when `.npz` missing.

**PR3 (driver integration) — complete**

**Commit:** `553f2df` on `feat/w3-triage-driver`

**New file: `datum_local/triage.py`**
- `TriageDecision` dataclass: `action`, `corrective_prompt`, `repaired_spec`, `reason`
- `ACTIONS` constants: `retry`, `retry_with_repair`, `park`, `escalate`
- 16 per-class corrective prompt builders — the key fix: `red_target_not_written`, `red_syntax_error`, `green_never_wrote` all produce truthful templates; only `red_deleted_tests` gets the DELETED-tests text
- Each RED/GREEN template embeds the S0 VERDICT block (`pytest_verdict_block`) when available
- `triage(failure_report, ledger) -&gt; (TriageDecision, updated_ledger)` — the O5 seam

**Driver wiring (all 9 exit paths):**
1. **Preflight** → `_classify_and_record` stamps `preflight_failed`/`HARD_STOP`
2. **Reset** → `_classify_and_record` stamps classification
3. **RED escalated+rejected** → routes through `triage()` instead of always `return 1`; `rules_tampering` still parks immediately, others get one triage shot
4. **RED retry exhausted** → `_classify_and_record` stamps classification
5. **RED retry loop** → `triage()` selects per-class corrective prompt (fixes the lying DELETED-tests bug)
6. **GREEN retry exhausted** → `_classify_and_record` stamps classification
7. **GREEN retry loop** → `triage()` selects per-class prompt with exact failing test IDs
8. **eedom infra error** → classified as `EEDOM_INFRA`/`ENVIRONMENTAL`
9. **eedom blocking after retry** → classified as `EEDOM_BLOCKING_AFTER_RETRY`/`HARD_STOP`

**Correction template improvements:**
- `red_target_not_written`: "file was NEVER written" — no more false DELETED claim
- `red_syntax_error`: SyntaxError + fence rule — no more false DELETED claim  
- `red_deleted_tests`: DELETED template + VERDICT block — only class where this is true
- `green_never_wrote`: "never modified" + VERDICT block — truthful
- `green_tests_still_failing`: exact `node_id` + `assert_line` per failing test + VERDICT block

**TriageDecision shape:**
```python
@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)
class TriageDecision:
    action: str           # "retry" | "retry_with_repair" | "park" | "escalate"
    corrective_prompt: str | None
    repaired_spec: str | None   # repaired red_prompt for RETRY_WITH_REPAIR
    reason: str
```

**Test delta:** +36 new tests in `tests/test_triage.py`; 1 existing assertion updated in `test_failure_persistence.py` (PR1 had `failure_class is None`; PR3 stamps it)

**Suite result:** 795 passed, 2 deselected, 0 failures. `ruff check` clean on all touched files.</result>

Key Strategies to Break Down TasksThe Plan-and-Solve Method: Ask the LLM to generate an outline or workflow before generating the actual content.Example prompt: "I need to write a comprehensive marketing strategy. First, break this project down into 5 key phases and list the specific objectives for each phase."Chain-of-Thought (CoT): Force the model to show its work or process information step-by-step.Example prompt: "Break down how you will solve this problem. For each step, explicitly state your reasoning, your planned actions, and the expected output before moving to the next one."Recursive Breakdown: If the LLM generates a sub-task that is still too broad, ask it to decompose that specific step further.Example prompt: "Break down Step 3 into three smaller micro-tasks, and let's work on the first micro-task together."Modular Workflows (Multi-Step Prompting): Instead of relying on one prompt, separate your process into distinct tasks. For instance, have one LLM prompt perform research, a second draft an outline, and a third write the final copy.

<result>Perfect. Now I have the complete picture. Let me create a comprehensive diagnosis:

## Diagnosis

**Root causes of 0/5 passing specs:**

1. **Sub-ask is too sparse and lacks testability constraints** (line 316-319 in `floor/author.py`)
   - The sub-ask is constructed as: `"a function named {name} with signature {signature} that {description}"`
   - Example: `"a function named register_command with signature register_command(name: str, ...) that stores a command spec and returns it"`
   - This gives the model ONLY the function name + signature + one-sentence behavior, with **no concrete examples** of valid inputs, edge cases, or expected outputs
   - The model then tries to invent test cases and a green sketch from this meager context, creating a mismatch

2. **Decompose prompt lacks testability and error-case guidance**
   - `DECOMPOSE_FUNCTIONS_PROMPT` requires "one sentence — what it does and what it returns" in the `description` field
   - But it provides **zero guidance** on edge cases, error conditions, type constraints, or expected boundaries
   - The model's one-sentence descriptions (like "stores a command and returns True") don't capture the contract richness needed for the proposal step
   - When the proposal step fills test cases, it invents edge cases (empty strings, None, invalid types) that weren't specified and may not match the green sketch it generates

3. **Literal verification gate exposes the mismatch but can't fix it retroactively**
   - `literal_check.verify_literals()` executes the proposal's test cases against its green sketch in an isolated subprocess
   - When tests fail (15/15 failures for `extract_command_metadata`, 3/5 failures for `generate_ui_panel`), it means the model wrote assertions that its own code doesn't satisfy
   - The one literal re-ask in `author.py` (line 485–497) shows only the failing tests, not **why** they failed — the model doesn't know what assumptions it violated
   - The correction message is vague: `"Fix the test cases OR the green_sketch so they agree"`

4. **Safety rejection on syntax errors (register_command)**
   - The AST safety gate (`check_ast_safety()` in `literal_check.py`) parses the test file and rejects on `SyntaxError`
   - This happened because the model generated syntactically invalid Python in the green sketch or test assertion
   - The safety rejection (`syntax/parse error: invalid syntax (&lt;unknown&gt;, line 9)`) gives **no context** about which line or what was invalid

**Concrete actionable improvements:**

1. **Enrich sub-ask descriptions with explicit contract constraints**
   - Instead of just passing `func.description`, include:
     - 1-2 concrete input examples (expected types, valid ranges)
     - Expected return type and value
     - 1-2 edge cases to handle (empty/None/invalid inputs)
   - Change line 316–319 to append structured behavioral bullets from the decompose step if available, or inject a template prompt that asks for these details

2. **Add contract-coverage rules to DECOMPOSE_FUNCTIONS_PROMPT**
   - Add a `behavior` field (like the main PROPOSAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT) requiring:
     - "list 3-4 behaviors: normal case + 2 edge cases + expected exceptions"
   - Enforce this in the decompose output validation

3. **Improve the literal re-ask feedback loop**
   - Attach the **actual test output** (not just the failing test names) to the correction message
   - Include a snippet of the green sketch around the failing assertion
   - Ask the model to reason about the mismatch before fixing

4. **Add pre-decompose validation to check descriptions for vagueness**
   - After the decompose step, filter out functions with descriptions shorter than ~20 words or that lack concrete details
   - Re-ask the decompose step to expand sparse descriptions before moving to proposal authoring

**Why this matters:** The decompose step generates sparse function descriptions that lack the behavioral richness the proposal step needs to write matching test cases and green sketches. The proposal step then invents test assertions and implementations from this terse context, creating a mismatch that literal verification catches but can't repair without more structured guidance.</result>

<summary>Agent "Research task decomposition for small LLMs - academic/industry patterns" completed</summary>
<result>Here are the concrete findings — all sourced from 2024-2025 papers:

---

**Root cause of your 0/5 failure: co-generation in a single JSON blob**

ConTested (ISSTA 2025) documents this exact failure mode. When tests and implementation appear in the same output, the model anchors on whichever field it writes first and lazily mirrors the other. Your single-blob JSON is the direct cause.

---

**1. Plan-and-Solve: pre-inject, don't self-generate**

CodePLAN (arxiv 2403.13271): 130%+ improvement on pass@1 — but only when the plan was *given* to the model, not generated in the same call. Small quantized models can follow a plan; they can't reliably make one simultaneously. Pre-populate a behavior narrative (expected I/O, edge cases) upstream before any code generation call.

**2. Contract-first, but split the calls**

ConTested: generate implementation first, then generate tests in a second call with the implementation in context. +32.9% over GPT-3.5, +16.97% over GPT-4o. Never co-generate. This is your biggest lever.

**3. Field order in the JSON schema is load-bearing**

COTTON (arxiv 2312.05562): put a `"reasoning"` scratchpad field at the *top* of your schema, before `signature`, `tests`, and `green_sketch`. Force the model to commit to expected behavior in prose before any code fields. 4-bit quantized models drop reasoning steps before dropping syntactically correct tokens — so if reasoning comes last, it gets skipped.

**4. Constrained decoding: necessary but insufficient**

JSONSchemaBench (arxiv 2501.10868): XGrammar and llguidance have near-zero overhead; Guidance leads on 6/8 benchmarks. Worth adding for parse reliability — eliminates malformed JSON. But it does not fix semantic drift between tests and code. Add it after you fix call-splitting.

**5. 10-15 test cases is past the quality cliff**

"Same Task, More Tokens" (ACL 2024) and Context Discipline (arxiv 2601.11564): quality degrades sharply with generation length for small models. Lazy generation — echoing earlier fields into later ones — is the exact mechanism behind your test/code disagreement. For a 30B 4-bit model, stay under ~800 tokens total per function. That means 3-5 test cases per call, not 10-15. Run multiple calls for more coverage.

---

**Ranked action list:**

1. Split into two calls — implementation first, tests second with implementation in context
2. Add `reasoning` field at the top of your JSON schema
3. Cap at 4-5 test cases per call
4. Pre-inject behavior narrative — no same-call planning
5. Add XGrammar or llama.cpp GBNF grammar for parse reliability</result>

<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Research practical small-model decomposition from practitioners" completed</summary>
<result>Here are the concrete, actionable findings:

---

## 1. Two-Pass Generation: Which Order?

**Code-first wins, hands down.** The research consensus (Tam et al., EMNLP 2024; assertion generation literature 2024-2025) is clear: committing to assertions before writing code forces the model to anchor on a conclusion it hasn't reasoned through yet. The effective pattern:

- **Pass 1**: Generate `green_sketch` only, free-form or with minimal structure.
- **Pass 2**: Feed the implementation back to the model and say "write test assertions that validate this exact function." The assertions become *derived from* the code rather than speculative.

The "AssertionBench" and "TOGLL" papers (2024) found that models generating assertions *against known implementations* dramatically outperform those generating assertions and code jointly. CodeT (Google, 2023) popularized this as "generate test cases as a verification property" — still the practical gold standard for local models in 2025.

**OSS tools**: `outlines` + a two-call pipeline is the simplest local implementation. No single OSS tool wraps exactly this two-pass flow, but it's trivial to implement manually with your existing OpenAI-compatible API.

---

## 2. Few-Shot Examples

- **1-2 examples is the practical sweet spot** for "fill this JSON form" tasks. More than 3 wastes tokens and can cause the model to pattern-match the example values rather than generate novel content.
- **Format that works**: Pair a minimal natural-language description with a complete, valid JSON response in the `messages` array (user/assistant role format). The example JSON must be exact — wrong key casing or wrong type in the example will propagate.
- **Key rule**: Put your JSON schema in the system prompt AND include one concrete filled example. The schema alone is insufficient for small models; the filled example teaches the shape more reliably than the schema.
- For code-heavy fields like `green_sketch`, include a short but real code snippet (not `"..."`) so the model understands that field contains executable Python, not a description.

---

## 3. Temperature and Sampling

The Kaitchup analysis (300 hyperparameter combinations on quantized models, partially paywalled) confirms: **4-bit quantized models are significantly more sensitive to temperature than their full-precision equivalents.** Quantization error acts like an effective temperature bump of ~0.1-0.3, meaning a 4-bit model at `temperature=0.7` behaves like a full-precision model at `temperature=0.8-1.0`.

Concrete recommendations for JSON output from a 4-bit quantized model:
- **Temperature**: `0.01` – `0.3` for structured/deterministic fields. The DataLeadsFuture article on Qwen3 AutoGen agents uses `temperature=0.01` for structured output tasks — that's the practitioner consensus.
- **Avoid `top_p` for JSON tasks**; use `min_p=0.05` instead (ICLR 2025 results show it outperforms top_p, especially preventing garbage token runs in JSON strings).
- **Repetition penalty**: Keep it at `1.0` or off for JSON. Repetition penalty will corrupt JSON by penalizing repeated structural tokens like `{`, `"`, `,` which appear constantly in valid JSON.
- **Do NOT set `max_tokens` when using constrained/structured output** — the Qwen3 docs explicitly warn that a truncated JSON string causes parsing failure.

---

## 4. Output Length and Decomposition

**Yes, small models degrade on long outputs.** The "Format Tax" paper (arxiv 2604.03616, 2025) and "Hidden Cost of Structure" (RANLP 2025) both confirm structured output requirements substantially degrade performance, with degradation compounding as output length increases.

For your ~6KB JSON proposal form, the practical fix is decomposition:
- **Request `green_sketch` in one call** (code generation mode, no JSON wrapper, just extract the code block).
- **Request `test_cases` in a second call**, with the `green_sketch` from pass 1 included in context.
- Optionally merge into one JSON object client-side.

This sidesteps the "commit to answer before reasoning" failure mode AND avoids long-output degradation. The Qwen3 MoE architecture specifically benefits from this — shorter focused generations use fewer active experts per token, reducing drift.

---

## 5. Self-Consistency / Verify-Then-Fix

Your existing single re-ask is the baseline. Practitioners have found better patterns:

**Best-of-N with execution verification** beats self-consistency voting for code tasks. The flow:
1. Generate 3 candidates at `temperature=0.3-0.5` (enough diversity without chaos).
2. Run the code in a sandbox against the assertions.
3. Pick the candidate with the most passing assertions (not "most similar to others").

For local inference where you want to avoid 3x token cost, a **2-pass verify-then-fix** works: generate once, then pass the code + assertions + failure description back and say "the assertion on line N fails when called with X — fix the implementation." Small models are better at *targeted fixes* than at getting it right the first time.

The PAG (Policy-as-Generator) pattern from 2024 shows a single model alternating policy/verifier roles works if you explicitly switch the system prompt between roles — don't ask the same prompt to "check its own work."

---

## 6. Qwen3-Specific Quirks

**Critical bug**: When thinking mode (`/think` or `enable_thinking: true`) is active AND you request JSON schema response format, `&lt;think&gt;...&lt;/think&gt;` reasoning content leaks into `message.content`, breaking JSON parsing. This is a confirmed open bug on the Qwen3.5-35B-A3B HuggingFace discussion board. Workaround: **disable thinking mode for structured output calls** (`enable_thinking: False` via `extra_body`), or strip `&lt;think&gt;...&lt;/think&gt;` blocks before parsing.

**For your pipeline specifically**: Use `/no_think` (or `enable_thinking: False`) for the JSON-filling pass. Use `/think` (or default) for the `green_sketch` free-form code generation pass. This aligns with the two-pass approach above — thinking mode for reasoning, no-think for form-filling.

**oMLX already strips think-spans** per your reference notes, so the leak may already be handled on your end. But confirm it strips from the `content` field, not just `reasoning_content`.

**Context window**: Qwen3-30B-A3B supports 256K context natively, so context length is not your bottleneck. The MoE architecture (8 active out of 128 experts) means it's faster than a dense 30B but reasoning quality per active parameter is lower — shorter, focused prompts extract better quality from the active expert subset.

---

## Summary of Immediate Actions

1. Split your proposal generation into two sequential API calls: code first, tests second (feed code into second call).
2. Set `temperature=0.1`, `min_p=0.05`, no `repetition_penalty`, no `max_tokens` for JSON calls.
3. Use `/no_think` mode for JSON-filling, `/think` for `green_sketch`.
4. Use 1 complete filled example in your prompt (not schema alone).
5. If assertion mismatches persist: add an execution-based verification step — run `green_sketch`, compare actual output to asserted output, feed the diff back for a targeted fix.

<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Research targeted code edits for LLM pipelines" completed</summary>
<result>Here's what the research found — concrete and focused on what actually works:

---

**Search/Replace Blocks: The Practical Winner**

Aider's search/replace format is the most reliable approach for small models. The model outputs fenced blocks with the exact old text and new replacement — no unified diff formatting required. Aider's docs (https://aider.chat/docs/more/edit-formats.html) show this format outperforms unified diffs for smaller/quantized models. Failure modes: whitespace mismatches and indentation drift. Mitigation: fuzzy matching on the search block (normalize whitespace before matching, not after).

**For Qwen3 30B 4-bit specifically**: Search/replace beats unified diffs. Unified diff requires the model to produce `@@` headers, line counts, and `+`/`-` prefixes — one formatting slip breaks the entire patch. Search/replace just needs two fenced blocks.

---

**Append-Only for TDD GREEN Phase**

For the specific TDD use case (adding a new function to make a test pass), append-only is 95%+ reliable and sidesteps the edit problem entirely. The model outputs only the new function body. You detect the insertion point programmatically (e.g., end of file, or after the last `def`). No search/replace ambiguity. This should be the default for the GREEN phase when the test failure is "function not found."

---

**Structured JSON Edit Instructions**

Works well when combined with constrained decoding (Outlines, llama.cpp grammar mode). Schema: `{"action": "replace", "search": "...", "replace": "..."}`. The explicit structure reduces parsing overhead. Without constrained decoding, small models sometimes wrap JSON in markdown fences or add commentary — you need a robust JSON extractor either way.

---

**AST-Based Edits: Research Only**

IDECoder and CODESTRUCT parse to AST and let the model specify node IDs. Structurally sound but no OSS tooling ships this for Python yet in a practical form. Not worth pursuing now.

---

**Bottom Line for Your Driver**

Recommended upgrade path, in order of implementation cost:

1. **Append-only first** — detect "missing function" failures, output only the new function, append it. Zero edit complexity, handles the majority of TDD GREEN cases.
2. **Search/replace blocks** — for modification cases (fixing an existing function). Prompt format: `&lt;&lt;&lt;&lt;&lt;&lt;&lt; SEARCH\n&lt;old code&gt;\n=======\n&lt;new code&gt;\n&gt;&gt;&gt;&gt;&gt;&gt;&gt; REPLACE`. Fuzzy-match the search block (strip trailing whitespace per line before comparing).
3. **Skip unified diffs entirely** — not worth the reliability cost for a 30B quantized model.

The arXiv paper 2506.22776 confirms 4-bit quantized models hold up well on code generation tasks, so the bottleneck is edit format compliance, not raw capability. Simpler formats win.</result>
